# Lead decisions on spec cards

Cards ask the lead to choose where the reference behavior is unclear, risky or not wanted. The rulings are here, one row each. A card that mentions a decision says "Decided" and points to this file.

Default rule: any "lead decision" in a card that is not listed below takes the card's recommended option, or the reference behavior when the card gives no recommendation.

Guiding rule for parity: Larsal opens every file the reference opens when a real tool, sync service or accident can produce it. Larsal is stricter only where a file needs the key holder's deliberate crafting, or where the reference behavior weakens security.

## Sprint 0 rulings (2026-10-25)

| ID | Card | Question | Ruling | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LD-01 | FMT-01.20, FMT-06.12 (D-06-1), FMT-07.11 (D-07-1), FMT-17.15 | Bytes after the final block, or after the logical end of the file | **Ignored, as the reference does.** They are never read as content. Larsal never writes them. Overrides the cards' proposal of Corrupt. | A sync tool that rewrites a file in place without truncating it leaves old bytes at the end. The reference still opens that file, so Larsal must too. The bytes are outside the checked data. Cuts and swaps inside the block stream still fail (AT-A03). |
| LD-02 | FMT-08.7 | Bytes after the gzip member, inside the checked payload | Corrupt (card proposal) | Only the key holder can put them there. |
| LD-03 | CRY-01.10 | Bad padding in version 4 with valid block HMACs | Corrupt (card proposal) | Only the key holder can make it. |
| LD-04 | CRY-01, E2-S4, AT-B07 | "Bad padding and wrong key give the same error" | The cipher API returns one error for any decrypt failure. In files, padding is checked only after integrity passes, so nothing about padding is visible without the key. E2-S4 and AT-B07 are read this way. | Version 4 finds a wrong key at the header HMAC, before any decryption. |
| LD-05 | FMT-01.10 | Unknown minor version (4.x or 3.x, x of 2 or more) | Open without a notice, as the reference does. Changed by LD-96: the unlock view shows a notice first. | Parity. Unknown document parts are kept (SD-06). |
| LD-06 | FMT-01.11, FMT-07, FMT-17.22 | Version 2.x files | Open with the version 3 reader, as the reference does. | Parity. |
| LD-07 | FMT-16.21, FMT-16.22 | Where the reference would write 3.1 | Larsal writes 4.0 with the version 4 AES-KDF, same rounds, fresh seed (card recommendation). The screen tells the user once that the vault moves to the newer format (APP card). | Larsal never writes version 3. The unlock time stays the same. |
| LD-08 | FMT-16.19 | History fields that need 4.1 | **Lossless: write 4.1** when any history item holds a 4.1 field. Overrides the card default. | Open, save, open again keeps every piece of user data (architecture, larsal-format Must). |
| LD-09 | FMT-16 (edge case) | Kept unknown document parts from a 4.1 or newer file | Write at least the version the file was read as. Overrides the card default. | Never claim an older version than the content it carries. |
| LD-10 | FMT-17.8, CRY-01.17 | Field stream id 3 in a version 3 file | Unsupported (card proposal) | No tool writes it. |
| LD-11 | FMT-17.19, FMT-05 (D-05-2) | Version 3.1 file without a header hash | Accept, as the reference does. | Parity. |
| LD-12 | FMT-05.11 (D-05-1) | Wrong key in version 3 | A start-bytes mismatch is WrongKey, the only version 3 WrongKey case. | Same meaning as the reference. |
| LD-13 | FMT-04 (D-04-1) | Open with no credentials at all | (a) Refuse with NoCredentials before the KDF. | No KDF cost, no misleading wrong-key message. |
| LD-14 | FMT-03.16, FMT-04.20, CRY-02.12 (D-04-2) | AES-KDF seed that is not 32 bytes | (a) Unsupported. | The reference silently skips all key stretching for such a seed. |
| LD-15 | CRY-03.4 | Salt length on save | Always a fresh 32-byte salt. | Stricter, and costs nothing: every save already runs the KDF again. |
| LD-16 | CRY-03.7 | Random size-padding item in the vault custom data | Write the same item the reference writes (same key, stored encoded), with a fresh value on every save. | Same privacy (file size differs on every save). The reference already handles that item correctly: it replaces it and never merges it. |
| LD-17 | CRY-02.19, FMT-03 | Argon2 version 0x10 in a file | Saved as 0x13. | The newer reference computes 0x13 anyway, and every save runs the KDF again. |
| LD-18 | CRY-02.23 | Default KDF for new vaults | Argon2id, 64 MiB, parallelism min(4, cores), tuned to 1 second (plan/security.md). | RFC 9106 recommends Argon2id. |
| LD-19 | CRY-02.28, E2-S5, AT-B06 | Benchmark accuracy | The tuned KDF takes 1.0 to 1.2 times the target. | Never weaker than promised. Covers both checks. |
| LD-20 | CRY-01.19 | Ciphers offered for new vaults | AES-256 (default), ChaCha20 and Twofish-256, as the reference offers. | Parity. |
| LD-21 | FMT-02.21 | Unknown outer header fields | Dropped on save, as the reference does (card proposal). | An unknown outer field could change how a payload must be read. |
| LD-22 | CRY-02.14, FMT-01.31, FMT-08.12 (L08-1, and the per-card limits it absorbs), FMT-11.48 (L11-4), FMT-18 (D-18-3) | Size and cost limits | The proposed numbers are accepted. Revisit if a demo vault, or a vault a user reports, passes one. | Every read is bounded (Rust rule 5). |
| LD-23 | FMT-09 (L09-1) | Version 3 header field 8 missing or empty | Corrupt (card proposal) | No usable field stream without it. |
| LD-24 | FMT-09.14 (L09-2) | Protected values inside kept unknown elements | Take no keystream bytes, as the reference does. | Parity: any other choice garbles every later protected value. |
| LD-25 | FMT-09 (L09-3) | Leading byte order mark in a protected value | The card default, which matches the reference. | Both apps show the same values. |
| LD-26 | FMT-11 (L11-1) | Duplicate group or entry ids | Mirror the reference merge. A duplicate that would put a group inside itself is Corrupt (the reference hangs there). | Parity, without the hang. |
| LD-27 | FMT-11 (L11-2) | Where unknown elements are kept | The card's current rules. | Already reviewed. |
| LD-28 | FMT-11.31 (L11-5) | Several History blocks in one entry | Keep only the last block's items, as the reference does. | No real writer makes this. Parity. |
| LD-29 | FMT-13.13 (L13-3) | Same attachment name twice in one entry | Keep both. The second gets the name FMT-13.13 gives. | No data lost. |
| LD-30 | FMT-18 (D-18-1) | Key file document with root `KeyFile` and a DTD | (a) Refused. | No DTD, ever (plan/security.md, Extra hardening). |
| LD-31 | FMT-18.21, CRY-03.14 (D-18-2) | Which key file kind Larsal creates | (a) XML 2.0 only, 32 random bytes, extension `.larsalkey`. | It has a check value, and both apps read it by content. |
| LD-32 | FMT-18.7 (D-18-4) | Text encodings Larsal reads in key files | (a) The eight decoders the card lists. | Covers every form seen in practice. |
| LD-33 | FMT-10, MOD-12.9 | When `SettingsChanged` moves | MOD-12.9's list (the merge needs it); FMT-10.24 points to it. | File format unchanged. |
| LD-34 | FMT-01.26 plan (c) | Demo vaults the command line tool cannot make | Verify agents may make them with the reference app's window, using a throwaway settings folder and demo passwords. Never the real settings folder. | Covers the 3.1 Twofish and ChaCha20 cells. |
| LD-35 | FMT-01.26 plan (e) | A version 3 vault builder for tests | Allowed: test code in `tests/`, built from FMT-17, never shipped. | Covers the 3.0 and 3.1 cells no tool makes. |
| LD-36 | FMT-01.26 plan (L) | 3.0 cells with no demo vault of their own | They count as covered when every layer is tested in other cells. | Each layer is still proven. |

## Sprint 1 rulings (2026-10-25)

| ID | Card | Question | Ruling | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LD-37 | CRY-01.11, E2-S4, LD-04 | Wrong key or IV length on decrypt | `KeyLength` and `IvLength` stay separate errors; `Decrypt` covers every failure that depends on the data. | Lengths are public and checked before any cipher work, so they tell an attacker nothing. |
| LD-38 | CRY-02.14 (estimate), CRY-02.2 | The argon2 crate runs lanes one after another (its thread feature needs a crate that is not approved) | The time estimate counts one thread: I × m × rec_ms / (rec_I × rec_m). The card's T25b numbers for 8 processors change to match. Adding threads is a later crate review (G6), tracked for the E2-S7 gate. | The card's formula assumes lanes run in parallel. On one thread it under-estimates by up to the processor count, so a 35 s unlock could start without asking. |
| LD-39 | Rust rule 3, CRY-02 security notes | Argon2 working memory is `Zeroizing<Vec<Block>>`, not `SecretBytes` | Allowed for private work buffers inside larsal-crypto that a crate needs in its own type. They must be wiped on drop, never cloned, printed or returned. | The argon2 crate needs `&mut [Block]`, and no unsafe code may turn a byte buffer into blocks. |
| LD-40 | CRY-02.28, CRY-02.14, AT-B06 | Benchmark run disturbed by other work on the Mac | At the count where one run first takes 50 ms, run twice more and keep the fastest time, both for the benchmark and for the speed record. | A slowed run gives too few iterations, a weaker KDF than promised. The Checkers measured 0.74 times the target under load. |
| LD-41 | CRY-01.10, FMT-17 (version 3 payload) | Version 3 needs the last block without a padding check; the decryptor gives it only through the padding check | The version 3 read story in E3 adds a decryptor end call that returns whole blocks with no padding check. Not built before that story. | Not needed until E3 reads version 3. |
| LD-42 | FEA-09.2, SD-07, INT-10 | Plain export from the command line | `larsal export` prints the not-encrypted warning on standard error, every time. | SD-07: every plain export warns. Standard error keeps the export output clean. |
| LD-43 | FEA-09 CSV (W1) | Values starting with `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` can run as spreadsheet formulas | Values stay exact (round trip). The CSV export warning adds one line: a spreadsheet may run values that start with these characters. | Changing values would break import; the user is told. |
| LD-44 | FEA-05.1 (W2) | The strength estimate ignores the entry's own title, user name and site | Pass the title, user name and URL host to the estimator as user inputs. | A password equal to the user name must not rate strong. Ratings only get stricter. |
| LD-45 | FEA-08.37 (W3) | OPVault item check value not verified on import | Verify it; on mismatch skip the item and list it in the import report. | Category, trash flag and times are then checked too. |
| LD-46 | FEA-08.16, L08-7(g) | Bad quoted CSV field on import | Larsal keeps the character the reference drops (default rule). | No text lost. |
| LD-47 | APP-09, APP-05 edge cases, APP-01 D-01-4 | Single instance | Larsal always runs one copy; there is no setting. If the running copy does not answer, Larsal shows M09-05 ("does not answer; quit it and try again") and exits 1 with no window. If the instance lock cannot be made (M09-06), Larsal refuses to start with a message saying what failed. | Two copies could write one vault and one settings file at once (SR-09). |
| LD-48 | APP-09.5, APP-11.7, APP-01 | Environment variables for the settings paths | Larsal reads none. The command line flags cover tests. | One documented way in; nothing can redirect settings silently. |
| LD-49 | APP-04.16, APP-01 D-01-6, AT-K03 | Keychain cleanup after a crash, at quit and when quick unlock is turned off | Match quick unlock items by service `app.larsal.vault` plus the label `Larsal quick unlock`, never by service alone. The shared-groups identity has its own account and label. | Deleting by service alone would destroy the user's signing identity. |
| LD-50 | APP-02.16 (D-A02-5), APP-05.28, SR-16 | An automatic lock that cannot save, or meets a changed-on-disk question | Option (d): Larsal writes a recovery copy (a normal encrypted vault with the same key, mode 0600, in Larsal's settings folder), then locks. At the next unlock Larsal offers Merge or Discard and then deletes the copy. If the recovery copy also fails, Larsal still locks, and the next unlock says that changes since the last save were lost. A manual lock keeps the question. | SR-16: a question never stops an automatic lock. An unattended Mac must not stay unlocked. |
| LD-51 | APP-01 (remember key files) | Default for remembering key file paths | Off. The setting stays. | The path next to the vault path tells a thief which file is the second factor. |
| LD-52 | APP-05.14 | The question after an atomic save fails | Default button Cancel. "Turn off" applies to this vault only. | One failure must not turn off safe saving for every vault. |
| LD-53 | MOD-12.10, APP-05 backups | After a key change, old backups and recovery copies still open with the old key | The key change notice says so and offers to delete Larsal-made backups and recovery copies of this vault. New parity row. | The user knows the old key still opens old copies. |
| LD-54 | APP-01 settings import | A settings import that weakens security (turns off locks or clipboard clearing, turns on "never ask" browser options) | Larsal lists those changes and applies them only after the user confirms. | No silent weakening. |
| LD-55 | APP-04.17 | Time limit on quick unlock | None, as the reference: the quick unlock key is removed when the vault closes or Larsal quits. | Parity; the window already ends with the session. |
| LD-56 | MOD-01.9, FMT-08.12 | Edit limits that let a save fail | Edit caps fit the file limits: values up to 50,331,648 bytes (48 MiB, so base64 stays under 64 MiB), names up to 64 MiB, at most 99,995 custom fields (the writer adds 5 standard ones). | The user must never make a vault Larsal cannot save. |
| LD-57 | MOD-04.14, FEA-01.21, INT-05, SD-05, SI-06 | Recycle bin entries and group flags | Bin entries are never searched and never Auto-Type candidates, whatever any group flag says. INT-05 repeats this. | The reference checks only inherited flags, so a group inside the bin with search or typing on leaks deleted entries. |
| LD-58 | MOD-08.9 (D-08-11) | Host form shown for internationalized names | Option (b): always the ASCII (punycode) form. | Simplest, and no look-alike host can pass as another. |
| LD-59 | MOD-08.31 (new), MOD-01, FMT-14, SR-11, AT-E02 | Who owns "open entry URL" | MOD-08 owns it (new rule MOD-08.31 with the reference facts). Larsal goes further: dangerous schemes never open (AT-E02). No question when every referencing entry is in the same delete. | One owner; the three pointers stay right. |
| LD-60 | MOD-11.19, MOD-11.20 (D-11-5), AT-A09 | Vault settings in a merge | Same-vault settings apply only when both start groups are roots, never for a share import or the import wizard. A merge never lowers the cipher, KDF cost or protection flags: the target keeps its value and a Conflict is recorded. The source's key is taken automatically only in the merge after a change on disk; elsewhere only after the preview says so and the user confirms. Browser association keys copy only in a same-vault merge. | A share or an older copy must not weaken or take over the vault. |
| LD-61 | MOD-11.4, .7, .11, .15, SI-09 | Share import scope | A share import matches ids only inside the target start group. An id found elsewhere is not merged and gets a Conflict record. | Otherwise a share can pull an outside entry into the shared group, where the next export leaks it. |
| LD-62 | MOD-13.13, MOD-13.1 (D-13-2), FMT-10.10, FMT-10.11 | The force-once flag | Confirmed as metadata element 28 (boolean, written only when true, after the force flag); FMT-10.11 lists 28 names. On a merge tie the flag is kept if either side has it. | Otherwise a key change can never clear it; a tie must not drop a forced key change. |
| LD-63 | SI-07, INT-06 | Turning the SSH agent feature off | Larsal removes every key it added, not only those marked for removal. It never clears keys other programs added. | Turning a feature off must not leave keys behind. |
| LD-64 | SN-02 | Website icon requests | Larsal drops any user name and password in the URL and never sends the entry's path. Icons stay off until the user turns them on. | Nothing secret leaves the Mac in an icon request. |
| LD-65 | MOD-06, SN-02 (icons from vaults and downloads) | Hostile icon images | Icons are decoded in Rust with size limits and re-encoded as PNG before the screen sees them. PNG, JPEG and ICO are accepted; any other or broken image shows the default icon and is kept unchanged in the vault. Needs an image crate (AT-J03 review) in E4. | No untrusted image bytes reach the web view; no data lost. |
| LD-66 | MOD-07.6, FEA-01 | Sidebar tag search | The sidebar hands over an exact tag term. | No escaping rules for users to learn; backslashes in tags cannot change the search. |
| LD-67 | SR-14, Rust rule 9 | Vault content in logs | No vault content (titles, user names, URLs, notes, field names) in any log, also in debug builds. | Logs leave the vault's protection. |
| LD-68 | MOD-02.22, MOD-03.10, MOD-11 | Smaller model questions | Copy into another vault clears the previous parent. The 4,000,000 item limit is checked at save and at open. A merge keeps the source's older location time, and a merged-in newer version keeps LD-09 (write at least the version read). | Card recommendations. |
| LD-69 | E2-S7 gate (Checker M2, M6, M7) | Crypto gate leftovers | (1) blake2 is not added as a dependency: the stack wipe after `derive` covers its state (probe: nothing left). (2) E3 decrypts payloads with the streaming `Decryptor`, never one-shot `decrypt`, so secret memory stays at one copy. (3) Argon2 stays on one thread through E3; the thread question is reopened in E5 with measured unlock times. | Smallest safe choices; each is measured, not assumed. |
| LD-70 | MOD-08.31, SR-11 | A vault field that says "run this command URL without asking" (`_EXEC_CMD` = `1`) | Larsal ignores it and asks every time, unless the user ticked "Remember my choice" with Yes on this Mac; that choice lives in Larsal's settings, keyed by vault, entry id and a hash of the exact command. The field is kept unchanged in the file. | Anyone who can change the vault (sync, shared group, import) could otherwise make one click run a program. |
| LD-71 | FEA-01.35, MOD-08.31, MOD-11.19 | Choices made while applying LD-56 to LD-68 | Confirmed: exact tag terms match case included; Larsal opens only `http`, `https` and `mailto` links, other schemes show a message (users can still copy the link); a switch between 256-bit ciphers is not a lowering in a merge. | Security first; no data lost. |
| LD-72 | CRY-02.28, AT-B06, F-026 | Benchmark on a busy Mac | `benchmark` takes a clock from the caller. Larsal passes the thread's CPU time, so other work cannot make the tuned KDF weaker. The calibration for cost estimates keeps wall time (a busy Mac only makes it ask more). | Wall time counts time the thread was waiting, so the red team got 0.5 times the target under load. |
| LD-73 | SI-07, SI-12, SD-11, SD-13 (E1-S7 Verify) | Reference weaknesses found in the second parity search | Larsal bounds the bcrypt-pbkdf round count of SSH key files (INT-06); answers the bridge's vault-hash request only for an associated client (INT-02); asks before opening an attachment in another app (MOD-05.12); attachment previews never follow a link without a confirm, and their images go through the Rust decoder (LD-65). | Each closes a way a hostile file or a same-user process gets more than it should. |
| LD-74 | FEA-09.10, INT-04.55, rule 2 | Passkeys in HTML exports; copied text in the mockup | HTML exports leave passkey fields out (INT-04.55 wins). The mockup's message strings are replaced with the cards' own texts; the look is unchanged. | Private keys stay out of plain files; no copied text anywhere in the folder. |
| LD-75 | plan/architecture.md, Rust rule 3 | larsal-model must hold protected values in secret types, but depended on no crate of ours | larsal-model depends on larsal-crypto, for its secret types only. larsal-crypto still depends on nothing of ours. | One set of wiped secret types; dependencies still point down. |
| LD-76 | FMT-10.16, MOD-04.1, FMT-11.35, FMT-13.13 (E3-S6 Builder) | Reader choices | (1) A vault whose recycle bin reference is the root group keeps the value in the file; Larsal acts as if there is no bin (E4). (2) Duplicate entry ids with attachments: keep both, the second renamed per FMT-13.13 (LD-29). (3) A group tree that duplicate-id moves make deeper than the depth limit is `TooLarge`. (4) A random-source failure while reading (a fresh id) maps to `Io`. | No data lost; every walk stays bounded. |

## Sprint 2 rulings (2026-10-25)

| ID | Card | Question | Ruling | Why |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| LD-77 | FMT-01 edge cases, FMT-02.7, FMT-02.25 (E3-S2 Builder) | The edge table said Corrupt for a wrong version stamp; FMT-02's rules give other kinds | FMT-02's rules win. A 3.1 stamp on a version 4 file is Unsupported (unknown cipher id, FMT-02.7). A 4.0 stamp on a version 3 file is TooLarge when a misread length passes the header limit (FMT-02.25), else Corrupt. The FMT-01 table now says so. | One source for each rule; the table only shows examples. |
| LD-78 | FMT-01.32 "spy" rows (E3-S4 Builder) | May production code keep a test counter? | Yes: `payload::probe` stays. It is hidden from the docs, counts KDF, decrypt and gunzip runs per thread, holds no data and changes no result. | Proves the "never ran" rows without a separate test build. |
| LD-79 | FMT-04.17, FMT-18.5 (E3-S3 Builder) | `Credentials` takes the key file's read result, not its raw bytes | Accepted. The caller reads the vault, then the key file (streamed), then calls `open_payload`. | A 1 GiB key file is read with flat memory, a save keeps the same key file value, and the FMT-05.7 order holds. |
| LD-80 | FMT-08.12 (E3-S6 Checker m3) | Is stray text directly inside a container element held to the text limit? | Yes. All direct text of an element counts toward the text limit, containers included. | One rule with no exceptions; FMT-08.12 already says "all direct text". |
| LD-81 | FMT-10.27 (E3-S6 Checker m4) | Kept unknown elements keep their layout whitespace; who drops it? | The reader drops it. Whitespace in an element that holds only child elements is not kept in the model. | The same document, indented or flat, gives the same model, so round-trip checks in E3-S7 and E3-S11 compare like with like. |
| LD-82 | MOD-12, plan/architecture.md `vault.rs` row (E3-S1 Checker M2) | Cipher, KDF and compression live only in the format's `Header`, not in the model | The model holds them, as the architecture and MOD-12 say: a typed encryption settings value in `Vault`, filled by the reader from the header and used by the writer. Built with E3-S7. | Vault settings (E4), merge (LD-60) and the writer all need them without carrying the file header around. |
| LD-83 | FMT-18, FMT-08.12, AT-A12 (E3-S3 fix pass, R2-B1/R2-B2) | New reading limit for key file documents | `key_file_attributes` = 1,024 attributes per tag, and every gathered key file token (name, namespace value, declaration value) is capped at 1 MiB. Over the cap inside a key file document is TooLarge; before the root is known the file falls back to the other key file forms. Each attachment pool item is still copied once out of the decompressed buffer; accepted until a streaming inner-header reader is needed. | Keeps time linear and memory flat for a crafted key file (80,000 attributes: 4.8 s before, 2.7 ms after; 128 MiB name: 946 MB peak before, 145 MB after). Real key files have at most a handful of attributes. |
| LD-84 | FMT-08.7, FMT-06, AT-A11 (Sprint 2 Verify) | The installed reference accepts a broken gzip trailer at 16 KiB boundaries, and a version 4 file with its final block removed; and (Sprint 2 Red team) an element with more than 128 namespace declarations in scope | Larsal stays strict: all three are `Corrupt`. Differential fuzzing (AT-A11, E3-S10) lists them as known differences, not failures. | A damaged file must never open silently; being stricter than the reference loses no valid vault, because the reference always writes the trailer and the final block. |
| LD-85 | FMT-08.12, F-036, F-037 (Sprint 2 Red team) | Memory while reading is not bounded by the limits: about 45 bytes per decoded byte for values built from many small pieces, and 1.4 to 2.1 KB per empty entry or history item | Two fixes. (1) A value is built in one buffer as it is read: a wiping buffer that wipes the old one each time it grows; no list of pieces. (2) A new limit `model` = 1 GiB in the FMT-08.12 table: every model object read (group, entry, history item, field, tag, attachment ref, custom data item, association, icon, deleted object, kept unknown element) adds its in-memory size plus its text bytes to one counter, and passing it is `TooLarge`. The count limits stay as they are. F-036 stays High and blocks the epic until re-tested. | Bounds worst-case memory to the document, the attachments and 1 GiB of model, whatever the mix of counts; real vaults (100,000 entries with history) use a small part of it. |
| LD-86 | FMT-11.35, FMT-08.12, F-038 | Duplicate entry ids merge attachments and history past the per-entry limits | The per-entry limits apply to the merged entry: passing one while merging is `TooLarge`. | Larsal never accepts what it would refuse to write. |
| LD-87 | FMT-18.10, F-039 | The key file "unsupported version" error carries raw file text | The error keeps the version text only when it is at most 16 characters of digits and dots; otherwise it holds no text. | No file-controlled text, line breaks or direction marks reach the screen through an error. |
| LD-88 | LD-67, Rust rule 9, F-040 | Derived `Debug` of the vault types prints notes, tags, custom data, auto-type sequences and field names | `Debug` of `Vault`, `Group`, `Entry` and the opened vault prints ids, counts and flags only, never user text. A test checks that no demo text appears in the `Debug` output of rich-v4. | Logs and crash reports can never carry vault content, even by mistake. |
| LD-89 | FMT-08.12 `model`, LD-85 (Sprint 2 red team fix pass) | At 1 GiB, a large real vault (100,000 entries with 10 history items each) uses about 0.92 GiB before any text, so it could be refused | The `model` limit is 2 GiB, and an entry or history item is charged its measured cost (1,400 bytes; the type sizes alone gave 808, which let real memory reach 3.5 GB at 2 GiB). Real memory for the model stays under 3 GiB at the limit (test `f037_entry_elements_limit_memory`). The per-type costs live next to the limit in `limits.rs`. | Refusing a real vault is worse than a fixed memory ceiling; 2 GiB leaves room for vaults twice that size, and crafted files still stop at a known ceiling instead of 5 GB or more. |
| LD-90 | FMT-08.12, FMT-13.15, LD-83, LD-89, F-041 (Sprint 2 red team re-test) | The memory limits stack: payload buffer, pool copies and model together reach 4.8 GB from a 5 MB file | (1) Version 4 pool items stay as ranges in the decompressed payload; an item is copied out only when a `Ref` first uses it, and unused items are never copied (they are dropped anyway, FMT-13.15). This replaces the copy LD-83 accepted. (2) Attachment content held in the model (pool items copied out, version 3 `Meta/Binaries`, inline values) is charged to `model`. (3) Groups are charged their measured cost, as entries are (LD-89). Goal: peak memory while reading any file stays under 3.5 GB in a release build, proven by the red team test `f041_memory_budgets_stack`. | One ceiling for the whole read instead of three limits that add up; unused content costs no memory. |
| LD-91 | LD-90, F-041 (Sprint 2 fix pass) | A genuinely large file (a 950 MB vault with an incompressible pool plus a large model) peaks at 4.45 GB, over LD-90's 3.5 GB goal | Accepted. When it runs out, the rest of the run matches the stored text without resolving, and the screen shows the existing limit notice. Goal: any one run finishes in under 250 ms in release on 10,000 entries, whatever the vault; a typical query stays under 50 ms. (2) The lazy DFA gives up sooner on small texts, and its cache work is charged. The fuzz target checks the budget accounting, with a wall-time guard only for real hangs (scaled for sanitizer builds). (3) The live-entry lookup for references uses one index per run. (4) G2 in check.sh also runs the release-only timed tests. (5) The 16-term cap of LD-124 counts every term that becomes a pattern (plain, wildcard or regex). (6) For E4-S11: the strength estimator caps its input. (7) Confirmed from the fix pass: once the whole meter is empty, the remaining checks count as not matching, and the notice is shown (stored text alone can be huge); resolutions may use only the first two thirds of the meter. (8) Accepted after the F-046 re-test: the 250 ms goal is set on 10,000 entries; on a 2 GB model of 100,000 entries x 200 fields a plain term took 245 ms in one run (median 192 ms). The list indicator, `is:weak`, statistics and the Health Check share it; the session fills it off the screen thread (E5). (2) In a search, a cached rating costs the meter like a stored-text check; an estimate made during the run is charged its real cost with a length-based model calibrated on the crate's measured release time. When the meter runs out, LD-126 (7) applies (the rest count as not matching, the limit notice shows). Goal: cold cache, 10,000 entries of 100-character passwords, under 250 ms with the notice; warm cache, full result under 50 ms. (3) The `strength_estimate` fuzz guard is scaled for sanitizer builds, as LD-126 (2). (4) The meter while typing runs off the screen thread and only after typing pauses; the Health Check and statistics run in the background with progress and cancel on lock (E5-S7 requirement; one estimate can take 200 ms). (5) `Check::findings` drops entries excluded since the run (FEA-06.9). (6) `Check::requests` and `answer` use indexes, not scans. (7) The hash-file record's 20 hash bytes are wiped. (8) `pattern.rs` builds with `dfa(false)` and `auto_prefilter(false)`, so vault patterns keep the engine set reviewed in LD-122. (9) Tests for online count saturation, an excluded entry offline (D-06-9) and the skip path of FEA-06.11. (10) Card wording: the security notes of FEA-05 and FEA-07 say reuse is compared in place (no password map). | Search stays inside its budget whatever the passwords are; each Checker item is closed or handed to the story that owns it. |
| LD-138 | AT-J03 (E4-S11 Checker) | Review of zxcvbn and the crates it adds | Signed by the Checker; lead co-signed 2026-10-29 (F-117; under LD-221 these become our own copies in vendor/) 2026-10-26. None has a build script or proc macro; cargo-audit and cargo-deny pass. zxcvbn 3.1.1: MIT, 1 owner, 13.2M downloads, released 2026-04-24 (latest); approved as the patched copy of LD-136, with F-048 closed by the zeroing allocator (LD-133) before release and a diff review at every version change (including its `.unwrap()` on fancy-regex results). fancy-regex 0.18.0: MIT, a team, 274M downloads, 0 `unsafe`; approved only through zxcvbn's three fixed patterns on at most 100 characters; Larsal's own code never uses it (LD-110 stands). bit-set 0.8.0, bit-vec 0.8.0, itertools 0.14.0, either 1.19.0, lazy_static 1.5.1: approved (the duplicate bit-set with proptest's dev-only 0.11.1 stays a cargo-deny warning). regex 1.13.1: approved with LD-137 (8), because its default features switch on dfa-build, perf-literal and the Unicode tables in the regex-automata Larsal uses (used `unsafe` 83 to 638 expressions). aho-corasick 1.1.5: approved as part of the regex family (LD-115), with the same pin. memchr 2.8.3: already in the tree. time 0.3.55 with deranged, num-conv, powerfmt, time-core: approved; used only by zxcvbn to read the current year. | The risks are in how Larsal uses zxcvbn (LD-136, LD-137), not in the crates. |
| LD-139 | D-05-1 (b), LD-136 (E4-S11 fix pass) | On the patched crate, (b) as written prices a repeat's base at the crate's 10 guesses per character: the FEA-05.19 300-character row falls to about 96 bits (Good, not Excellent), and the copy cut short by the 100-character input counts as new text | A repeat match counts its base by (b) plus log2 of the repeat count; matches right after it that spell the start of the base (a copy cut short) count as one more repeat. The cache of LD-137 is keyed by entry id and last-modification time, which has whole-second steps: the session (E5) calls `Ratings::forget` on every edit, merge and reload of an entry, and re-runs an `is:weak` search that showed the limit notice once the cache is filled. | 300-character row 126.5 bits (reference 127.9); the repeated 18-digit block 50.5 bits (the block alone 47.9); every other parity row unchanged. |
| LD-140 | D-05-1 (b), LD-136, LD-139 (E4-S11 Verify F-049, F-050) | The crate picks its match sequence by its own price (10 guesses per brute-force character and a penalty per extra match); Larsal then re-prices that sequence at log2(N) per character, so the two disagree. Phrases of common words joined by spaces or dashes rate higher than in the reference 35 of 40 times (`correct horse battery staple`: Good 80.1 in Larsal, Weak 70.9 in the reference), which skips the weak master password warning | Larsal picks the sequence by the same price it reports. E = the smallest sum over every way to cover the password with the crate's matches and single characters: a match costs log2 of its guesses (a repeat as LD-139, its base priced by this same rule), and a character outside a match costs log2(N), N from the classes of the whole password (the D-05-1 (b) classes). There is no penalty per match. The crate's own sequence search is no longer used for E; its match finder is reached through one more change to the patched copy (LD-136 change 5: `omnimatch` and `scoring::estimate_guesses` made public, nothing else; the second because omnimatch's matches carry no guesses until priced, confirmed by the lead after the fix pass). F-050: the change list at the top of the copy's lib.rs also names what was left out of the copy (README, CHANGELOG, benches, CI files, dev-dependencies and package metadata: not built, not shipped). | One price for choosing and for reporting, so a sequence can never be chosen cheap and reported dear. Done when, on Verify's 500-password set, word phrases with separators rate higher than the reference at most 5 of 40 times, `correct horse battery staple` is below Good, 05-27 passes, and E stays the same on every call. |

Larsal's rating is closer to the real strength, and the lower side only adds a warning. | LD-155 | README D3 screen layer | Owner 2026-10-27: "native and Rust, no Tauri, write in Swift"; "don't reinvent the wheel" (AGENTS.md rule 9) | Native macOS app: Swift screens (SwiftUI) over the Rust engine linked into the app as a library. Signal uses a hand-written C layer (libsignal swift/README). The Tauri shell of E5-S1 (LD-150 to LD-154) is withdrawn; the plan, architecture and E5/E6 stories are re-planned from a researched reference layout before any build. Secrets stay in Rust; only the one Show value crosses to Swift. | The owner's choice; UniFFI is what proven apps like Larsal use. |
| LD-156 | README D3, LD-155, AGENTS.md rule 9 (E5-S1 re-plan) | The native app's layout and standards | From plan/native-app-research.md (sources per point there). (1) Layout: one bindings crate `crates/larsal-ffi` (was `larsal-desktop`), the only crate that exports to Swift; `crates/uniffi-bindgen`, pinned to the same UniFFI version; `macos/` with the Xcode project, the Swift sources and the local package `LarsalCore`; one repository, as Signal. (2) Build: `scripts/build-core.sh`: static library, `uniffi-bindgen-swift` in library mode, xcframework, Swift package; UniFFI `=0.32.2`, proc-macros only; host target only, the x86_64 slice and `lipo` wait for E14 (the target is an install); generated files are not kept. (3) Xcode: checked-in `macos/Larsal.xcodeproj`, synchronized folders, shared scheme `Larsal`; scripts set `DEVELOPER_DIR` to Xcode when unset; XcodeGen only as a fallback, with the owner's yes. (4) Swift: Swift 6, main actor as default isolation, macOS 14; `swift format lint --strict` and SwiftLint `--strict`; SwiftLint is a check tool under the E0 install permission (rule 7), pinned with a SHA-256 checksum, installed by the Builder in E5-S1; Swift Testing for unit tests, XCTest UI tests for G8; plain SwiftUI, one `@Observable` view model per screen; no snapshot library (AT-K05 covers the look). (5) Logging: `larsal-ffi` sends `tracing` events, values dropped, to a Swift `LogSink` that writes to `os.Logger`; no `tracing-oslog`. (6) Secrets: Session and Vault are UniFFI Objects; list views carry no secret; Show returns the one `String` that crosses; Copy is a Rust call; stricter than the studied apps (rule 4). (7) macOS features: clipboard, Touch ID Keychain and hardening in Rust `larsal-platform`, shared with `larsal-cli`; lock signals, idle time, `sharingType`, secure input, biometry availability and Finder open in Swift; the APP-09 socket stays in Rust. E6-S3 needs a signing team (Apple TN3137): a free personal team needs the owner's Apple ID in Xcode (owner item), signed builds need D7. SR-04's AES-256-GCM is decided at E6-S3 start from proven apps (rule 9): `aes-gcm` with AT-J03, or the approved primitives. (8) Distribution: hardened runtime, no App Sandbox, Developer ID, notarized and stapled (E14-S3); release entitlements `keychain-access-groups` only. (9) Checks: G1 adds Swift format and SwiftLint, G2 adds `build-core.sh` and `xcodebuild test` without UI tests, G8 runs `LarsalUITests`; AT-E04 and AT-E05 retired; `tauri`, `tauri-build` and `objc2-local-authentication` leave the approved table; a Rust panic ends the app (fails closed, accepted). (10) Kept from LD-150 (6): the zeroing allocator in E6-S6; the thread CPU-time clock in E5-S8, supplied by `larsal-ffi`. | The common standard of proven apps with a Rust core and native Apple screens (rule 9); one repository because Larsal ships one Mac app. |
| LD-157 | AT-J03 (E5-S1 Checker) | Review of `uniffi` 0.32.2 and the crates it brings; `libc` 0.2.191 (approved table: "AT-J03 at the E5-S1 Checker") | Signed by the Checker 2026-10-27 with conditions; lead co-signed 2026-10-29 (F-117). **New crates:** 23 through `larsal-ffi` and 28 more in `crates/uniffi-bindgen` only (`cargo tree -e all`, compared with every other crate of ours). **Licenses:** MPL-2.0 for the 8 `uniffi*` crates (exact-version exceptions in deny.toml, used unmodified, so MPL-2.0 asks only for the notice and a source link); every other new crate MIT or Apache-2.0 (`weedle2`, `goblin`, `nom`, `scroll` MIT). **Maintenance:** a Mozilla project used by the proven apps in LD-155; `uniffi` 0.32.2 is the latest release (2026-09-23), 15.5M downloads; `anyhow` 1.0.104 (1.1B), `bytes` 1.12.1 (1.19B), `camino` 1.2.6 (304M), `fs-err` 3.3.2 (152M), `askama` 0.16.1 (57M), `static_assertions` 1.1.0 (568M, last release 2019, compile-time asserts only), `weedle2` 5.0.0 (Mozilla, 2024, generator only), `goblin` 0.8.2 (generator only; 0.10.7 exists). **What is linked:** `liblarsal_ffi.a` from `scripts/build-core.sh` holds only `uniffi`, `uniffi_core`, `anyhow`, `bytes` and `static_assertions` of the new crates (`ar t`; 0 generator objects); `uniffi_meta` is build time only (inside `uniffi_macros`), and `uniffi_pipeline` with its `tempfile` and `fastrand` is declared by `uniffi` but not linked. In Larsal.app (`nm`): Release arm64 `uniffi_core` and `anyhow` only, Debug also `bytes`. No `__mod_init_func` or `__init_offsets` section in either binary: no code runs before `main`. **Build scripts:** `anyhow`, `camino` and `fs-err` probe the compiler only (`rustc --version`, a test compile in OUT_DIR). **Proc macros:** `uniffi_macros` reads the crate's own Cargo.toml and Cargo's environment, nothing else; `uniffi_internal_macros`; generator only `askama_macros`, `clap_derive`, `scroll_derive`. **Reach:** no network code in any `uniffi*` crate; the generator runs `cargo metadata` and reads the built library; `uniffi_pipeline`'s debug printer can run `diff` (generator only, not linked). **Unsafe:** `uniffi_core` holds the boundary's real `unsafe` (130 uses: buffers, foreign bytes, handles, futures). The scaffolding in `larsal-ffi` (macro expansion, nightly `-Zunpretty=expanded`): 57 `#[unsafe(no_mangle)]` items, 55 `extern "C"` functions (51 of them `unsafe extern "C"`) and 2 metadata statics, no `unsafe` block; rustc does not apply `unsafe_code` to other crates' macro output, which is why `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]` compiles. Hand-written `#[unsafe(no_mangle)]`, an `unsafe` block inside a `#[uniffi::export]` body, one from a local `macro_rules!`, and `#[allow(unsafe_code)]` are each refused (probe, restored). **Advisory:** RUSTSEC-2026-0374 (`basic-toml` 0.1.10, "unmaintained", no fix) is reached only through `askama_derive`, inside the `askama_macros` proc macro of `uniffi_bindgen`: compile time of the generator, never in the app (`cargo tree -p larsal-ffi -i basic-toml` finds nothing); the ignore is accepted. **libc** 0.2.191: MIT or Apache-2.0, rust-lang, latest (2026-10-16), 1.86B downloads; build script probes the compiler; used only in `larsal-platform/src/macos/` (confstr, getpeereid, geteuid, signal mask and sigwait, tcgetattr/tcsetattr, uname), where Rust rule 2's two-Checker `unsafe` review applies. **Conditions:** (1) exact pins: `uniffi` `=0.32.2` and every `uniffi*` crate one version; a version change gets a diff review of `uniffi_core`, a new expansion count, and the `ar t` and `nm` checks above. (2) `larsal-ffi` keeps `uniffi` without features; `cli` and `cargo-metadata` stay in `crates/uniffi-bindgen`, and build-core.sh keeps its two separate cargo runs. (3) The RUSTSEC-2026-0374 ignore stays only while `cargo tree -p larsal-ffi -e normal --target aarch64-apple-darwin -i basic-toml` finds nothing; remove it when UniFFI drops `basic-toml`. (4) The release (E14) ships the MPL-2.0 notice and a source link for `uniffi` and `uniffi_core` and for the generated Swift glue (written from `uniffi_bindgen`'s templates). (5) A new crate whose macros write code into `larsal-ffi` gets its expansion checked for `unsafe`: the forbid does not see it. | AT-J03 needs the lead and a Checker in writing. |

| LD-179 | SIGPIPE in the window app; E9-S1 card gaps | E9-S1 Builder 2026-10-28: Rust ignores SIGPIPE only in Rust programs, the window app starts in Swift | (1) At start (APP-09.1 step 1) the window app sets SIGPIPE to ignore (`larsal_platform::macos::ignore_broken_pipes`), so a write to a closed socket (instance channel, SSH agent) is an `EPIPE` error, not the end of Larsal; the command line tool is a Rust program and already ignores it. Sources: Rust's standard library ignores SIGPIPE before every Rust program's main (and restores the default in children it starts); Node.js and Chromium ignore it process-wide too; signal(3). (2) Where INT-06 is silent: a lifetime above 999,999,999 seconds gives MI06-17; remove-all's reply 5 gives MI06-21, any other failure MI06-02; a file with several keys gives MI06-10. (3) The SSH key reading lives in larsal-crypto (rule 3), not larsal-sshagent. `ssh-key` stays at `=0.7.0-rc.11`, the newest release: there is no stable 0.7, and 0.6.7 is built on the older crypto generation that the version bans in deny.toml refuse (corrected by the E9-S1 Checker: no `p256` advisory applies); AT-J03 in LD-182. | A closed connection must never kill the app; crypto stays in one crate. |

| LD-193 | LD-133, LD-150 (6), LD-156 (10), APP-11.9, SR-07, rule 9 (E6-S6 build) | How the zeroing allocator and the key page locks are built | (1) A small wrapper of our own, no new crate: larsal-platform `macos/memory.rs` `Zeroing<A>`, the shape of the `zeroizing-alloc` crate, which Bitwarden's Rust core installs (sdk-internal `crates/bitwarden-crypto/src/lib.rs`: `#[global_allocator] static ALLOC: ZeroizingAllocator<std::alloc::System>`, a re-export of `zeroizing_alloc::ZeroAlloc`, in a library its iOS app links through UniFFI): wrap the system allocator, zero `layout.size()` bytes in `dealloc`, leave `realloc` to the trait default so the old block of a moved buffer also goes through `dealloc`. A crate would need an AT-J03 review for about 30 lines that the approved `libc` already covers. (2) The wipe is C11 `memset_s` (Annex K.3.7.4.1: never optimized away; libsodium's `sodium_memzero` uses it on macOS), one optimized libc call per free instead of a byte-by-byte volatile loop. (3) Installed in the `larsal` tool's `main.rs` and in larsal-ffi's `lib.rs`, so in the Mac app's static library, as Bitwarden does in its library. It covers Rust's allocations only (Swift and system libraries call `malloc` themselves), the UniFFI buffers that carry records to Swift included. Every binary that links larsal-ffi gets it, so nothing linked with larsal-ffi may bring a second global allocator. (4) Key pages: `memory::lock(&[u8]) -> Option<Locked>` locks the whole pages under a buffer with `mlock`, and dropping the guard calls `munlock`. macOS nests locks per page (mlock(2) on 26.6: "nested mlock calls"; checked: two locks and one unlock leave the page wired), so Larsal keeps no page count. Refused (RLIMIT_MEMLOCK, which Larsal never raises, or the system wired limit): M11-01 once, go on unlocked (APP-11.9). The same as libsodium's `sodium_mlock` and the locked pools of crypto libraries. No `madvise`: macOS has no per-range core dump exclusion; release builds turn core dumps off (APP-11.2). (5) Observed on this Mac (macOS 26.6, C probe): `malloc` zeroes small freed blocks itself but keeps the bytes of freed blocks from 4 KiB to 1 MiB and hands them back at the same address, which is where the gzip state of F-034 (43 KiB) and F-042 (33 KiB) lives. (6) Wiring (lead approval 2026-10-28): larsal-format holds every key it keeps in `keys::LockedKey` (a `SecretBytes` plus its page lock; dropped in that order: wipe and free, then unlock): `P`, `R`, `C` and `T` in the open and save key work, `K_enc` and `K_mac` (`FinalKeys`), the version 3 cipher key, the stream key (`InnerHeader`, and the fresh one of a save), `F` (`KeyFileKey`), and `T` in `TransformedKey`, which an unlocked vault keeps for its next save. larsal-crypto stays free of platform code: its functions return `SecretBytes` and the holder locks right after. Not locked: the `FieldStream` state (larsal-crypto, moved by value, alive only during a document read or write), the decompressed payload that also holds the stream key (large; the card asks key material only), the version 3 stream key copy in the plain header (it is in the file unencrypted). The quick unlock key comes with E6-S3. (7) larsal-format depends on larsal-platform with `default-features = false` and only the `memory` feature, which links libc alone; the clipboard, OS version and physical memory (objc2, Foundation, AppKit) are behind the `appkit` feature, which larsal-cli and larsal-ffi turn on (Verify 2026-10-28: keep the vault format lean). | Rule 9: the pattern of a proven Rust password manager core linked into a native app; one allocator closes every third-party copy at once. Cost (release, big-10000, median of 5): open 250 to 253 ms, save 324 to 331 ms (1 to 3 %). |

| LD-201 | AT-J03 (E9-S2 Checker 1) | Review of `p256` =0.14.0, `ed25519-dalek` =3.0.0 and the crates they bring (approved table: "AT-J03 at the E9-S2 Checker") | Signed by Checker 1 2026-10-28 with conditions; co-signed by the lead 2026-10-28. **New in Cargo.lock under the two (15):** through p256: ecdsa 0.17.0, elliptic-curve 0.14.2, primeorder 0.14.0, primefield 0.14.0, rfc6979 0.6.0, crypto-bigint 0.7.7, ff 0.14.0, group 0.14.0, wnaf 0.14.1, der 0.8.2; through ed25519-dalek: ed25519 3.0.0, curve25519-dalek 5.0.0 (build script), curve25519-dalek-derive 0.1.1 (proc macro), fiat-crypto 0.3.0, and the build-only rustc_version 0.4.1 (missing from the Builder's list of 14). num-traits 0.2.19 (build script through `autocfg`) was test-only and is now linked through crypto-bigint. curve25519-dalek-derive and fiat-crypto are target-gated (the x86_64 SIMD backend; the `fiat` backend setting) and not built on this Mac (`cargo tree` for the host does not list them). **Licences:** MIT OR Apache-2.0 for all but ed25519-dalek and curve25519-dalek (BSD-3-Clause, on the deny.toml allowlist) and fiat-crypto (MIT OR Apache-2.0 OR BSD-1-Clause). `cargo deny check`: advisories, bans, licenses, sources ok (the one duplicate warning, syn 2 and 3, was there before). **Maintenance:** each the newest release in the local crates.io index (copy of 2026-10-24), dates from their changelogs: RustCrypto p256 0.14.0 2026-07-03, ecdsa 0.17.0 2026-07-02, primeorder 0.14.0 2026-07-02, primefield 0.14.0 2026-06-23, rfc6979 0.6.0 2026-06-29, wnaf 0.14.1 2026-09-03, der 0.8.2 2026-09-05, elliptic-curve 0.14.2 and crypto-bigint 0.7.7 2026-10-24, ed25519 3.0.0 2026-05-03; zkcrypto ff 0.14.0 2026-05-27, group 0.14.0 2026-06-01; dalek-cryptography ed25519-dalek 3.0.0 and curve25519-dalek 5.0.0 2026-07-06. Download counts not looked up (no network). **Build scripts:** curve25519-dalek's reads Cargo's target variables and runs `$RUSTC -vV` through rustc_version to pick the backend (`serial`, 64-bit, on arm64); num-traits' probes the compiler through autocfg; neither touches other files, the network or other programs. **Reach:** no file, network, process or environment access in the linked crates (source search). **Unsafe:** none in p256, ecdsa and rfc6979 (forbidden), ed25519-dalek, ed25519, primefield, ff and wnaf (forbidden), primeorder, group, fiat-crypto; elliptic-curve 4 and der 4 (newtype reference casts under `deny(unsafe_code)`); crypto-bigint 14 (limb and slice reinterpretations); curve25519-dalek 1 on arm64 (a static table cast; its other unsafe is in the x86_64 AVX2 and IFMA backends, not built here); num-traits 1 (`to_int_unchecked` after a range check). **Constant time:** Ed25519 signing is constant-time (curve25519-dalek's basepoint multiplication on `subtle`); P-256 signing uses the constant-time generator multiplication, the constant-time scalar inversion and RFC 6979 nonces; the variable-time paths (`invert_vartime`, `lincomb_vartime`) serve only verification of public data. p256 and ecdsa state that they were never independently audited (accepted: the RFC 8032 and RFC 6979 vectors pass; crypto-bigint had an NCC audit of an older version). Signing keys wipe on drop: `ecdsa::SigningKey` always, `ed25519_dalek::SigningKey` with `zeroize` (on). **Conditions:** (1) exact pins `p256 =0.14.0` and `ed25519-dalek =3.0.0`; every bump gets a diff review and a rerun of the larsal-crypto and larsal-sshagent tests. (2) Features stay: p256 `ecdsa` only (no `pem`, `pkcs8`, `serde`, `ecdh`, `hash2curve`, `oprf`, `getrandom`); ed25519-dalek `zeroize` only (no `rand_core`, `pem`, `pkcs8`, `serde`, `hazmat`, `batch`, `legacy_compatibility`). (3) Used only in larsal-crypto `ssh.rs`: signing only for keys of Larsal's own agent after the user's Allow Once, verification only for session bindings. (4) No `curve25519_dalek_backend` or `curve25519_dalek_bits` setting (RUSTFLAGS, `.cargo/config`); an x86_64 build (Intel Mac or a universal binary) compiles curve25519-dalek-derive and the AVX2 backend's unsafe: reviewed then. (5) The one-version bans for these crates stay in deny.toml; E7's pinned `crypto_box =0.9.1` needs curve25519-dalek 4 (`^4` in the index) and will hit the ban: E7 takes a release on curve25519-dalek 5 or reviews a named skip. (6) The approved table names rustc_version and num-traits (done with this row). **Bump 2026-10-28 (E9-S2 Verify finding 1; AT-J03 diff note under condition (1)):** crypto-bigint 0.7.7 to 0.7.10 (2026-10-26) and ctutils 0.4.5 to 0.4.6 (2026-10-25), with `cargo update -p crypto-bigint --precise 0.7.10 --offline`; nothing else in Cargo.lock moved. Why: upstream yanked crypto-bigint 0.7.5 to 0.7.8 for a breaking change (#1373, the `Mul` impl of `NonZero<T>`), not for a security fix, and deny.toml's `yanked = "deny"` failed the check.sh deny gate. 0.7.10 and 0.4.6 are the newest in the local index and not yanked. Diff (`diff -r` of the two registry copies): crypto-bigint replaces the ctutils primitives it used with its own in `primitives.rs` (`borrowing_sub` with the borrow kept behind `black_box` before it becomes a mask, as ctutils does; `leading_zeros` and `trailing_zeros` use the CPU instruction on aarch64 and x86_64, a fixed-step software count elsewhere), keeps the Montgomery multiplication carry in branch-free arithmetic (#1376), uses `shr_vartime` on the public prime in `PrimeParams::new_vartime` (#1372), tightens the `NonZero<T>` `Mul` and `One` bounds to `Integer` (#1373), and adds tests; ctutils only adds `borrowing_sub_mask_u32` and `_u64` (#1577) over the same inner subtraction. No new dependency, no build script, no line with `unsafe` added or removed, no file, network, process or environment access. fuzz/Cargo.lock re-locked to the same versions (the E9-S2 crates added; `der` 0.8.2 and `elliptic-curve` 0.14.2 pinned with `--precise`): no package has a different version in the two locks. Proof: `cargo deny --offline check` advisories, bans, licenses, sources ok (with 0.7.7 put back in a private copy: `error[yanked]`, advisories FAILED); larsal-crypto, larsal-sshagent and larsal-ffi tests pass. | AT-J03 needs the lead and a Checker in writing. Co-signed by the lead 2026-10-28. The 2026-10-28 bump: the Checker of the E9-S2 fix signs the diff. |
| LD-202 | E9-S2 Checker 1 findings (Rust part) | Checker 1 2026-10-28 (6 findings, 4 notes; sent back) | (1) A sign request holds a strong reference to the decrypted key while it waits for its turn and while the question shows (`server.rs` `sign` and `ask`, `keyring.rs` `own_key`), so after a lock, a remove or a lifetime end the key of a locked vault stays in memory until that request's turn ends (up to 60 s for each question ahead of it), against INT-06.31; and a lock between the held check in `ask` and `open_question` leaves a question open for a key that is gone. Fix: hold only a weak reference until Allow Once, register the question only while the key is held (under one state lock), take the key right before signing. (2) Card gap, INT-06.34: the question names the host of the connection's last checked binding, but the data to be signed is never tied to that binding: any local process can connect to a known host itself, bind that session on Larsal's socket and get "‹program› wants to sign in to ‹known host›" for other data. Proposed: as OpenSSH's agent checks for destination-limited keys, Larsal names the host only when the data is a `publickey` user authentication request (RFC 4252 section 7; for `publickey-hostbound-v00@openssh.com` its host key equals the bound one) that carries the last bound session ID; otherwise the host is unknown (MI06-31). (3) A key file is read into a plain, growing buffer (`entry.rs` `read_file`): read it into a wiping buffer sized up front (Rust rule 3). (4) R06-6 (Return and Space do nothing for the first second; Escape and Deny at once) is noted nowhere for the Swift dialog: one line on `Asker::ask` and an open item on the board. (5) "Resolved as Copy resolves" depends on the caller's `Context`; the tests use `Context::new` (no hidden one-time password and passkey names, no LD-126 meter) and nothing tests LD-170 (4): `EntryKey::unlock` and `Keyring::add_on_unlock` say that the caller passes Copy's context (larsal-ffi `views.rs` `resolved`), best as one shared constructor in larsal-core, and the wiring adds a test that `{S:otp}` as the password gives MI06-14. (6) The real-`ssh` test reads the owner's own `~/.ssh/id_*` files (OpenSSH takes `~` from the user database, not `HOME`; `ssh -G -F /dev/null` lists them): add `-o IdentityFile=none` (ssh_config(5)). Notes: a panic in a client thread does not fail `hostile_clients_never_hang_or_panic` (count panics with a hook); `max_clients_one_more_is_closed_at_once` says idle clients get an answer but does not check it; `Keyring::lock` waits for an in-flight system-agent add (up to 2 s) before it wipes Larsal's own keys (drop them before taking the operation lock); the requesting program's name stays open, as the Builder reported. | Keys of a locked vault never stay in memory; the dialog names a host only when the signature is for it. |
| LD-203 | E9-S2 Checker findings: rulings | Checker 1 2026-10-28 (6 findings; LD-202 notes) | (1) Larsal's own agent holds only a weak reference to a key until Allow Once; the question is registered under the same state lock that checks the key is still held; the key is taken right before signing, so a lock, remove or lifetime end frees it at once (INT-06.31). (2) Card gap, ruled: the question names a host only when the data to sign is a `publickey` sign-in request (RFC 4252 section 7, or the host-bound form) carrying the session ID bound on that connection; otherwise the host is unknown (MI06-31), as OpenSSH's agent checks for host-limited keys; INT-06.34 gains the rule. (3) Key files are read into a wiping buffer with its size reserved up front. (4) The `Asker` docs carry R06-6 (Return and Space do nothing for the first second; Escape and Deny work at once). (5) One shared constructor in larsal-core gives Copy's resolve context (LD-170 (4) hidden fields and the LD-126 meter); larsal-sshagent takes it; the wiring adds a test that `{S:otp}` as a key passphrase gives MI06-14. (6) Tests that run real `ssh` pass `-o IdentityFile=none` and a throwaway user config, so the owner's `~/.ssh` files are never read (AGENTS.md rule 5). E7-S1: `crypto_box` is re-pinned to a release on the same `curve25519-dalek` generation as E9-S2, so the one-version ban in deny.toml holds. | No key or host claim outlives the user's answer; the owner's files untouched. Note 2026-11-01 (LD-299): E7-S1's `crypto_box` re-pin is dropped (LD-274 (1)); E7-S1 removes the Cargo.toml pin and its vendor/ copies. |

The strength line keeps the mockup's "level · whole bits" (FEA-02.15). | LD-216 | APP-05.24, plan/architecture.md "File change watch" (E5-S10) | Verify 2026-10-28: the disk watch is built in Swift with kqueue vnode sources (`DispatchSource.makeFileSystemObjectSource`) on the folder and the file, a 500 ms join and a 30-second tick, not with the `notify` crate the card and the approved table named; no row gave the choice and its sources (rule 9) | (1) The watch stays native in Swift (`macos/Larsal/Platform/DiskWatch.swift`): one vnode source on the real path's folder (`.write`: an atomic rename by a sync service or by Larsal, a delete, a move, a conflict copy beside the file) and one on the file (`.write`, `.extend`, `.delete`, `.rename`, `.attrib`: an edit in place), both opened with `O_EVTONLY` and opened again after each event, because after a rename the old descriptor watches the old file; events within 500 ms join into one; a 30-second timer covers what the system misses (a share that was away). (2) It only says "look": Rust compares the checksum and decides (`Session::check_disk`), so Larsal's own saves raise nothing. (3) No crate: `notify` leaves the approved table and the workspace pin (it was never linked; `Cargo.lock` has no entry). APP-05.24 and the table say so. | The reference watches with a system file watch plus a 30-second check (APP-05.24, from the source). Apple's File System Events Programming Guide: FSEvents report per folder after a latency (`FSEvents.h`: the stream's latency parameter), and its chapter "Kernel Queues: An Alternative to File System Events" is the mechanism for single files; Dispatch's `DISPATCH_SOURCE_TYPE_VNODE` and kqueue's `EVFILT_VNODE` with `NOTE_WRITE`, `NOTE_EXTEND`, `NOTE_DELETE`, `NOTE_RENAME`, `NOTE_ATTRIB` (macOS SDK `dispatch/source.h`, `sys/event.h`, `kqueue(2)`), `O_EVTONLY` "descriptor requested for event notifications only" (`sys/fcntl.h`). `notify` 8.2.0 on macOS wraps the same two mechanisms (its `macos_fsevent` default and `macos_kqueue` features, source read locally), so a Rust watch adds a crate and a callback across UniFFI into Swift for no new mechanism. Larsal's other OS events live in Swift on the same Apple mechanisms (LD-159 (2): quit signals as Dispatch sources; E6-S2 lock signals). |
| LD-217 | APP-02.16, APP-05.4, APP-05.28 T60 (LD-50) (E5-S10) | Verify 2026-10-28 probe V-C T60: an automatic lock or a quit signal dropped a changed vault's unsaved change when its save did not run, failed or met a file changed on disk; no recovery copy was written, and no story owned it | (1) E5-S10 writes the copy: Rust `Session::write_recovery_copy` (saving.rs) builds the vault as it is in memory under its current key and writes it with larsal-core's safe method at 0600 into `recovery` (made 0700) of Larsal's settings folder (the state file's folder; nothing for a memory-only store, as in tests and hidden launches), named by the SHA-256 of the real path in lowercase hex plus `.larsalvault`, a newer copy replacing the older; when that fails, an empty `.lost` marker (0600) beside it. Swift `saveAll(asks: false)` (every automatic lock: `LockSignals` → `lockAll()`; a quit signal, logout or shut down: `mayQuit(asks: false)`) calls it for each changed vault it did not save, then locks. (2) The next-unlock offer (`MSG-LOCK-RECOVERY` Merge or Discard, `MSG-LOCK-LOST`) has no Option A frame: asked as W-16; until then the copy stays on disk. (3) APP-02 T55 makes the `recovery` folder read-only, which also stops the `.lost` marker the card puts in that folder: Spec to look at T55. (4) Routed to E5-S10 and built 2026-10-28: the channel's lock request (APP-02.12, start.rs `Request::Lock`) is an automatic trigger (APP-02.16) and runs as `LockSignals` do. The sender waits at most 1 second for the answer (instance.rs `REQUEST_TIME`), so the channel answers at once and hands the lock to the window through the `LarsalEvents` callback `lock_all`; Swift `WindowEvents.lockAll` runs `MainWindowModel.lockAll()` on the main queue (the step 8 save by the settings, a recovery copy for each change it could not save, then the lock). With no window events set, Rust's `session::lock_all()` locks every vault at once, as before. | SR-16 and LD-50: an automatic lock never waits and never drops a change without a trace. |

| LD-221 | Dependency policy (AGENTS rule 3, plan/architecture.md approved table, deny.toml, E0-S4); story E0-S7 | Owner 2026-10-29: "We don't use any dependencies from other people and we write our own. We can copy other dependencies, adapt them to our app, and create libraries from them within our app, especially for encryption. We do not reinvent the wheel but we do not rely on dependencies." Today: 40 outside crates named directly, 255 outside packages in Cargo.lock, 2 already copied (vendor/zxcvbn LD-136, vendor/ssh-key LD-209) | (1) Every outside package becomes Larsal's own copy in the tree: `cargo vendor` into `vendor/`, source replacement in `.cargo/config.toml`, builds with `--offline --locked`; nothing is downloaded at build time; each copy keeps its license file and its version in the bill of materials (E14-S4). Proven practice: Firefox (third_party/rust), Chromium (third_party/rust), Android (external/rust) and Fuchsia keep every Rust crate in their own tree; cargo vendor is Cargo's own tool for it (sources: the E0-S7 research section). (2) Small packages where our use is a few dozen lines are replaced by our own code (candidates: thiserror, base64, hex-like helpers, getrandom's one call); each with tests. (3) Crypto copies stay byte-for-byte the audited upstream code: we cut unused parts and features only, never change the math (rule 3: no home-made crypto); known-answer tests (AT-B01) run on our copies. (4) Large non-crypto packages (UniFFI, rustls, quick-xml, flate2, png, regex, clap, objc2) are copied and trimmed to what we use, not rewritten. (5) A check fails when a build reaches any source outside the tree; security fixes are watched by `cargo audit` against our copies' versions, and an update is a reviewed copy. | E0-S7 builds it after E14-S4 (same build files); research starts now Addendum 2026-10-29 (E0-S7 research, spec/research/dependencies.md): (6) rustls and rustls-webpki are crypto copies (no code edits, cuts of unused files and features only), not copy-and-trim. (7) getrandom is Larsal's random source, so it stays a crypto copy trimmed to its macOS backend; no home-made replacement (rule 3 unchanged). (8) serde_json stays a copy-and-trim: it parses untrusted import files, and a new hand-written parser would be fresh attack surface; replacing it needs fuzzing equal to upstream's first. (9) Plain copies go in `vendor/crates-io/` (cargo vendor --versioned-dirs); the two patched copies stay in `vendor/zxcvbn` and `vendor/ssh-key`; an audit list covers path copies, which cargo audit skips today. |
| LD-222 | AT-J04, E14-S4, the third-party notices of E14-S2 (E14-S4 Builder) | How Larsal makes a reproducible release build, its bill of materials and the third-party notices | (1) `scripts/repro-build.sh` builds the `larsal` tool, `liblarsal_ffi.a` (`--features net`, as build-core.sh) and Larsal.app (Release) with `--locked`, the release profile unchanged (lto, codegen-units = 1, panic abort, symbols stripped: already deterministic), a fixed `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` (1793232000, 2026-10-29; the last commit time once git starts), `ZERO_AR_DATE=1`, `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0`, `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`, and every build path mapped to a fixed name: `--remap-path-prefix` of the tree to `/app`, cargo's home to `/cargo`, the target folder to `/target` (physical paths, since the linker and SwiftPM resolve links; the target folder last, as the last match wins), the same maps for C (`-ffile-prefix-map`, ring) and Swift (`-file-prefix-map`, both the `/private/tmp` form and the `/tmp` form Xcode writes), the linker's `-reproducible` (Xcode passes it for Larsal itself), and Larsal's executable stripped as an Xcode archive is (`DEPLOYMENT_POSTPROCESSING`, `STRIP_INSTALLED_PRODUCT`: the debug map held object paths; the dSYM keeps the debug info). Apple's linker (ld-27037.1, Xcode 27.0) lays out the `objc_msgSend$` selector stubs in a different order from link to link: the same link command on the same inputs gave two hashes in three runs. So Swift is built with `-Xfrontend -disable-objc-msgsend-selector-stubs` (direct `objc_msgSend` calls, as before Xcode 14); then five relinks gave one hash. `actool` races when it writes `Assets.car`'s appearance table: about one compile in ten (8 of 100 test compiles, also from one compile to the next in the same `ibtoold` daemon; `SWIFT_DETERMINISTIC_HASHING=1` reached the daemon and did not help) puts the two names (`NSAppearanceNameDarkAqua`, `NSAppearanceNameSystem`) in the other order: 24 bytes, all 225 renditions with the same digest (`assetutil --info`), and CodeResources and the executable's signature (32 bytes, the resource hash) follow. The script does not edit the file (no home-made normalizer of a private format): it reads the names, and when they are not in sorted order (the usual outcome) it builds Larsal again from an empty derived-data folder, up to 8 times, then runs `codesign --verify --strict` (Checker 1 fix). Deleting only the bundle's `Assets.car` does not work: Xcode 27 compiles the catalog into its intermediates and copies it into the bundle (`LinkAssetCatalog`), so an incremental build copies the same file back and does not sign again (a test that planted the swapped file left a broken seal). The Xcode project's own Release configuration is unchanged: E14-S3 makes the shipped build with this script. It fails when any output still holds the tree, the target folder or the home folder (the user's name), then writes SHA256SUMS of every file in `target/repro/`. Cargo's `trim-paths` is still unstable in 1.99.1 (`-Ztrim-paths`), so the maps are explicit. Fixed build path (Checker 1): the maps take a path out as text but not its length. Swift passes the unmapped file name's length to `swift_isEscapingClosureAtFileLocation` (a closure given as an Objective-C block), and the linker's UUID counts the length of the debug map's object paths (relinks from paths of the same length gave one UUID, a longer path another). So the script copies the tree to `/private/tmp/larsal-repro/src` and builds there with the target folder `/private/tmp/larsal-repro/target`, empty at the start and deleted at the end, one run at a time; the outputs go to the starting tree's `target/repro/` (or `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`'s). The `larsal` link also gets `-Wl,-S` (no debug map; `larsal` is stripped anyway): one of its object paths is the toolchain in the home folder, so the user name's length changed the UUID; with `-S` relinks from paths of three lengths gave one UUID. `BUILDINFO`, hashed with the rest, records the build path, `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`, rustc, cargo, Xcode, the macOS SDK and the macOS build: a rebuild on another toolchain or macOS build differs (Larsal's Info.plist records them), as the definition allows. (2) `scripts/make-sbom.sh` writes CycloneDX 1.6 JSON from `cargo tree` (aarch64-apple-darwin, normal edges; a proc macro counts, its own dependencies do not, as they only run at build time; plus `uniffi_bindgen`, whose templates are Larsal.app's Swift glue), `cargo metadata` and Cargo.lock: name, version, `pkg:cargo` purl, SPDX expression, the Cargo.lock SHA-256, authors, repository, which product ships it, the dependency graph and a timestamp from `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`. The patched copies of `[patch.crates-io]` (zxcvbn LD-136, ssh-key LD-209) carry `pedigree` (the upstream purl as ancestor, patch type `unofficial`, the Cargo.toml note) instead of a checksum; plain copies made by `cargo vendor` (LD-221) keep their crates.io identity and checksum. The Rust standard library is one component. (3) `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`: every shipped crate with license, product and source, then each license file (and AUTHORS file) once with the crates that ship it. Crates that publish no license file (today the objc2 and uniffi crates) get the standard text from `scripts/licenses/` (MIT: the Rust toolchain's SPDX text; MPL-2.0: the canonical text, SHA-256 fab3dd6b…) with their authors and source. The standard library's own notice is the toolchain's `COPYRIGHT-library.html`, copied beside it. (4) The notices and the bill of materials are release outputs in `target/repro/`, never kept in the tree: MIT requires each copyright line word for word, and one (rustls-platform-verifier's) names an outside product, which the name guard rightly refuses in this folder. Packaging (E14-S2, E14-S3) puts them in Larsal.app beside `Resources/LICENSES.md`. (5) No new tool: cargo-cyclonedx and cargo-about would be downloaded and built; `cargo tree`, `cargo metadata` and python3's standard library give the same fields. diffoscope is not installed; outputs are compared by SHA-256. | Reproducible Builds project: definition (reproducible-builds.org/docs/definition), SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH (reproducible-builds.org/specs/source-date-epoch), build paths (reproducible-builds.org/docs/build-path: build in one fixed path where a tool keeps it), Debian's `.buildinfo` (`deb-buildinfo(5)`: Build-Path and the installed toolchain), archive dates (reproducible-builds.org/docs/archives). The rustc book, "Remap source paths" (1.99.1 docs, rustc/remap-source-paths.html: last match wins; Apple OSO paths need `-oso_prefix`); Rust builds its own standard library this way (its paths read `/rustc/<commit>/library/...`). Cargo changelog 1.39 (#7459: RUSTFLAGS is not in the symbol hash) and reference/unstable.html (`-Ztrim-paths`). Apple `ld(1)`: `-reproducible`, `-oso_prefix`, `-S` (no debug information); cc-rs sets `ZERO_AR_DATE` for Apple `ar`. Security apps publish builds anyone can rebuild to the same hash: Tor Browser (blog.torproject.org, "Deterministic Builds"), Signal (Signal-Android `reproducible-builds/`). CycloneDX 1.6, ECMA-424 (cyclonedx.org/docs/1.6/json), and the CycloneDX project's own Rust tool cargo-cyclonedx (github.com/CycloneDX/cyclonedx-rust-cargo: `pkg:cargo` purls, Cargo.lock SHA-256); purl spec, cargo type (github.com/package-url/purl-spec); NTIA, "The Minimum Elements for a Software Bill of Materials" (2021). Notices: cargo-about (github.com/EmbarkStudios/cargo-about) lists each license text once with its crates; Firefox (about:license) and Chromium (about:credits) list every third-party part with its text. |

| LD-224 | APP-07.8, APP-07.10, M07-07, T08, T18; CRY-02.25, CRY-02.28; APP-02.16, APP-02.17, T55, T62 (E5 follow-up batch: the card checks of LD-211 (4) and LD-217 (3)) | Spec 2026-10-29: Custom writes the unlock time item where APP-07.10 and T18 said advanced mode writes none; the wizard scales one 1,000 ms count where APP-07.8 and T08 gave one formula; M07-07's items carry "(default)" and "(recommended)" the build and Option A do not show; T55's read-only `recovery` folder also stops the `.lost` marker, so its MSG-LOCK-LOST could never show | (1) **The unlock time item.** Option A's step 2 draws "Recommended" (1.0 s) and "Custom" (cipher, key derivation, an "Unlock time" slider, memory, lanes) and no typed count. Custom sets the count from the chosen time by Larsal's own measurement of that kind, memory and lanes, as simple mode does, so the time is known and the item is true. Larsal writes the item with the chosen time in both choices and for the vault settings' unlock time (MOD-12.11), keeps it when only the cipher changes, and removes it only when a count is set another way (a typed count; MOD-12.12 "Applying advanced mode removes the unlock time item" and MOD-12.22 "in simple mode" read so). The reference removes the item only after a typed count, whose time is not known until the KDF runs (source reading of its encryption settings page), and opens any vault holding the item on its basic page, keeping the KDF kind, memory and lanes; it listed `300` for a Custom vault (E5-S8 Verify B1). The build already does this (wizard.rs `edit_for`, vault_settings.rs `set_kdf`, `set_cipher`). (2) **Scaling.** The build measures once for 1,000 ms (N) and sets ceiling(N × target / 1,000) (wizard.rs `kdf_for`). With the benchmark's nanosecond time this is never below CRY-02.28's one-step count for that target and at most ceiling(target / 1,000) above it (exact-integer check of 300,000 random runs: worst +4 at 5,000 ms, 0 at 1,000 ms); a recount from the speed record would use its whole-ms time and be less exact. The cards now give the two-step rule. (3) **M07-07.** The items read "AES-256" and "Argon2id": a closed Mac pop-up list shows the chosen item's text (Apple HIG "Pop-up buttons"; AppKit `NSPopUpButton`), Option A draws "AES-256" and "Argon2id" on step 2 and the Security page, the default is the preselected item as in Apple's own lists (Disk Utility's Format list marks none), and the recommendation is in the "Recommended" text and the Security page's Key derivation note. The build already matches. (4) **T55 and the marker.** The running app is the trace that cannot fail; a file marker only bridges a restart, best effort (AppKit `NSDocument` keeps a failed autosave in memory and the running app presents "could not be autosaved"; browsers keep their crash flag in the profile's preferences file and lose it when that cannot be written). So the Rust session keeps a memory note of the loss, keyed by the copy's name (SHA-256 hex, no vault content, rule 4), set whenever an automatic lock or quit signal keeps a change in no copy (a memory-only store included); the `.lost` marker stays for a restart; no second place is tried, since a full disk or an unwritable settings folder stops every place alike. The next unlock (W-16) reads the note or the marker, and OK clears both. T55 now tests the read-only folder (no copy, no marker, the note, MSG-LOCK-LOST in the same run); new T62 tests the marker across a restart (the copy's name taken by a folder, as E5-S10 Verify V3-LOST did). Known ceiling: a copy and marker failure followed by a quit leaves no trace after the restart. (5) **Builder, in the batch.** (a) saving.rs: the memory note in `Session`, set when `write_recovery_copy` returns false (compute the name before the memory-only early return), with a read method for tests and W-16; the unlock-time read, MSG-LOCK-LOST and the clear wait for W-16. (b) Tests named by id: T55 (read-only `recovery`: locked, folder empty, note set); T62's lock half (marker 0600, 0 bytes, note set); T18 by key (Custom at 300 ms writes `300` under CRY-02.25's key, Recommended `1000`); T08 (fake measurement: ceiling(N × 2,500 / 1,000)). No change to the item writing or to M07-07's list texts. | Option A's Custom chooses a time, not a count; the running app keeps the trace a full disk cannot take away; nothing new waits on the owner. |

(2) **F-015 known limits accepted:** a raw single C1 byte in non-UTF-8 text is not followed (macOS terminals run in UTF-8, where xterm and VTE take C1 only as U+0080..U+009F, which the guard handles); CAN and SUB cutting a code short, other DEC parser quirks, a tab as one column, and a carriage return inside a file name are not modelled (following them means writing a terminal emulator). (3) **F-017 residual accepted:** an explicit `bash scripts/check-names.sh` with a hostile BASH_ENV or exported function runs before line 1, so no script can stop it; check.sh and every test run the guard through its #! line, which is safe; control of the environment is outside the threat model. | LD-228 | F-101 scope (LD-225 (1)), part g's known costs, F-123 to F-125, the spec cards for part g's rules (E13-S6 part g Checker 1 and Verify, 2026-10-29) | Lead 2026-10-30: part g signed (5 findings re-tested); its agents asked for rulings | (1) **The ruling holds to its words: no lock waits on a save on the main thread.** The automatic locks already wait at most 1 s; the menu's Lock Everything (asks:true; 3.86 s measured behind a slow save) also runs its save in a Task, as saveNow does, then asks or locks on the main actor; owner part i. A frozen window stops the idle lock too, so the user's own Lock must never be the slow path (AppKit's rule: no blocking work on the main thread). Quit keeps waiting for its save on purpose (a quit must not drop changes) and uses AppKit's `terminateLater` reply so the window does not freeze, in part i if small, otherwise noted as a known limit. (2) **Part g's known costs accepted** (bounded by the save, not by an attacker): a save the lock overtook keeps a vault copy and key parts in Rust memory until it ends; N stalled vaults wait N seconds; a lock save queued behind a stalled save of the same vault takes no copy of its own; with both save settings off, an automatic lock's writeRecoveryCopy waits on the save lock. (3) **New findings:** F-123 (Medium, check_writable follows a planted link) and F-124 (Low, ResourceBusy counted toward APP-05.14) go to part j; F-125 (Low, DiskWatch open without O_NONBLOCK) goes to part i.
