| LD-231 | INT-05.37 to INT-05.47, D-INT05-15, LD-186 (3) (E8-S3 typing on macOS) | Builder 2026-10-30: how Larsal turns a checked plan into key events, from proven open source Mac apps (AGENTS.md rule 9) | (1) **Where events are made.** Every key event, the ones that carry secret characters included, is made and posted in Rust (larsal-platform `macos/keyboard.rs`, CoreGraphics linked as a framework, no crate, LD-221): from a private event source (`kCGEventSourceStatePrivate`) with its flags set explicitly, key-down then key-up posted together to the login session's tap (`kCGSessionEventTap`), then the key pause. MacPass does the same (its Auto-Type command classes: a private event source, `CGEventPost(kCGSessionEventTap, …)`, key-down then key-up, a pause after each key), as Apple's Quartz Event Services documents (`CGEventSourceCreate`, `CGEventCreateKeyboardEvent`, `CGEventSetFlags`, `CGEventPost`). This closes D-INT05-15's crate choice: no bindings crate. (2) **Characters.** Normal mode: one Unicode-string event pair per character (`CGEventKeyboardSetUnicodeString`; both UTF-16 units of a character outside the Basic Multilingual Plane in one event), key code 0, no flags (INT-05.40), as MacPass's text path does and as Apple documents for characters a layout lacks (Quartz Event Services, `CGEventKeyboardSetUnicodeString`). Key-code mode and key combinations look the character up in one table Swift builds once per run from the current layout (`TISCopyCurrentKeyboardLayoutInputSource`, else `TISCopyCurrentASCIICapableKeyboardLayoutInputSource`; `kTISPropertyUnicodeKeyLayoutData`; `UCKeyTranslate` for each main-block key with no modifier, ⇧, ⌥ and ⌥⇧; dead keys, the keypad, controls and private-use characters left out; read on the main thread, as the Text Input Sources calls need), as MacPass's key mapper builds its character-to-key map from the same layout data; Rust keeps one key per character with the fewest modifiers (LD-186 (3)); a combination needs the plain form (INT-05.42). (3) **Timing and stops.** S-AT-10 (default 25 ms) follows every press; `{DELAY n}` waits run in 20 ms slices that end at once on a stop; the run waits on its own thread, never the main thread. The stop flag (a lock, lock-all, close, the shortcut again) is read before and again after Swift's window check, so a stop raised while an Accessibility call is under way (one call on a busy app can block for the messaging timeout, Apple `AXUIElementSetMessagingTimeout`) still stops that key. (4) **Rule 10.** Only Larsal's own, non-hidden launch calls `autotype_post_events` and installs the system permission (`SystemAccessibility`, whose `request` is macOS's own prompt, INT-05.44 at most once per Larsal run); the test bundle has no host app and does not build `Platform/Accessibility.swift`; every test types into the recorder and a fake permission. (5) **INT-05.47.** One debug line through `Logger.app` (private value, Swift rule 4): `Auto-Type stopped: ‹code› after ‹n› keys` (`not-in-front`, `keys-held`, `window-changed`, `stopped`, `no-event-source`) and `Auto-Type failed: ‹MI05 id›`; the reason text is not logged because it may hold the token. Card gap for Spec: larsal-autotype's errors carry no token position, so a failure line names the message id only. (6) **Not built here** (W-06 waits for the owner's mockup OK): Type Login from Larsal's window with MI05-50 (INT-05.33; so INT-05.38 step 2's single start delay has no second caller yet), the pick dialog (INT-05.36; a `{PICKCHARS}` plan returns `NeedsPicks` and types nothing), the shortcut recorder. (7) **Mac QA (owner's go):** T96, T97, T100, T101, T104 and V-INT05-1 to V-INT05-8 need the Accessibility permission for a test process and a target window; they run in one Mac QA session (V-INT05-3). | One Rust path makes every event; every test runs on fakes; the gaps have owners. |
| LD-232 | Part c2 Brotli limit, part f proposals (F-079, F-080, F-093, F-107), INT-05.32 owner, part f leftovers (E13-S6 part c2 Builder, part f fix round, E8-S3 Builder, 2026-10-30) | Lead 2026-10-30: rulings asked for while the runs waited on the session limit | (1) **Bare Brotli streams accepted as a known limit** beside raw deflate: Brotli has no magic number, so a stream outside a WOFF2 font and not named *.br cannot be found without trying every byte offset; WOFF2 fonts stay on the SHA-256 allow list (7 hashes cover the 14 files), so any new or changed font fails the guard until checked. zstd frames inside other files fall under the existing 'compressed streams inside other formats' limit. macOS's own libcompression could decode Brotli through ctypes later if a need shows. (2) **open_url stays stricter than the reference:** a URL whose resolution holds a protected value, or a URL field marked protected, is refused with MSG-08-4 and a protected `cmd://` shows masked. Rule 4: opening hands the value to Swift and then to another app; the reference opens them, Larsal does not until Rust can open the URL itself through larsal-platform (as Copy goes to the pasteboard from Rust, never through Swift). Parity gap noted, Low; no owner until a user asks. (3) **APP-06.24 reading confirmed:** a passed-in unlock uses the remembered slot and the challenge looks for that serial; with the key absent the attempt fails with the key's own message, and no separate USB look runs first. (2) **AT-J03, the `unsafe` block of `thread_cpu_time`** (larsal-platform `macos/system.rs`, the KDF benchmark's clock, LD-72). No new crate: `libc` =0.2.191 (LD-157; the approved table names `clock_gettime`). The one block calls `clock_gettime(CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID, &mut ts)`. Binding against the macOS 27.0 SDK: libc's `CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID: clockid_t = 16` and `fn clock_gettime(clockid_t, *mut timespec) -> c_int` (`clockid_t` = `c_uint`, `timespec` = `time_t` and `c_long`) match `_time.h` (`_CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID = 16`, `int clock_gettime(clockid_t, struct timespec *)`) and `sys/_types/_timespec.h`; the clock exists since macOS 10.12, Larsal's floor is 14.0. Memory: the pointer is to a local, initialised, aligned `timespec` that outlives the call; the call writes only it, keeps no pointer, and is thread-safe; nothing is read before the result is checked. A non-zero result panics (LD-156 (9): a stopped or guessed clock would tune a wrong KDF), and both fields go through `try_from`. The `// SAFETY:` note is there (clippy `undocumented_unsafe_blocks`). Tests: larsal-platform `thread_cpu_time_stands_still_while_the_thread_sleeps` and the larsal-ffi pin above. Builder's review; Rust rule 2 asks two Checkers for `unsafe`, so the batch's Checker 1 co-signs here. (3) **Text only.** M07-07: before, the card's items read "AES-256 (default)" and "Argon2id (recommended)"; after (LD-224 (3)), "AES-256" and "Argon2id", as the build draws them (NewVaultParts.swift `EncryptionRows.ciphers` and `.kdfs`, now pinned by `NewVaultChecksTests.listWords`). APP-01's count table, worked out from the card alone (no reference opened): before, Entry management 10 IDs for its 11 rows, total 174, and a reconciliation of 174 − 9; after, 11, Settings screens 104, total 175, and 175 − 10 (S-ENTRY-11 is Larsal only, so not in the defaults table) + 1 + 1 − 1 = 166, unchanged. S-UI-07's Larsal column reads "D: on (LD-212 (5))" and APP-01.2 lists it. The columns read from the reference source (controls defined, stored values) are not touched. (4) **Not built here:** reading the note or marker at unlock, MSG-LOCK-LOST and its clear (W-16); a size difference still gives compare.mjs no pixel or colour result, it now says so in every cell. | Each point has a test or a line; the `unsafe` block waits for its second Checker (Rust rule 2). Co-signed by Checker 1 2026-10-30: the binding read again in libc 0.2.191 (`apple/mod.rs`) and the SDK's `_time.h` and `_timespec.h`; the module denies `undocumented_unsafe_blocks`; a `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` mutant fails both clock tests. |

| LD-247 | E0-S7 step 1 (LD-221) and AGENTS rule 1: outside packages that hold a banned name | Lead 2026-10-30: a scan of the 254 locked registry packages (and the fuzz workspace's 3 extra) found a banned name in 6: rustls-platform-verifier (its MIT copyright line, README and a test domain name the product the owner's list forbids), windows-sys 0.52 and 0.61 and schannel (Windows API names), ring (a register name in an x86_64 assembly generator script, crypto/fipsmodule/aes/asm/aesni-x86_64.pl) and itertools (an identifier in src/duplicates_impl.rs whose first letters spell a banned word) | (1) **Rule 1 holds inside vendor/ too:** the name guard scans every vendored copy with no allow-list for banned names. (2) **rustls-platform-verifier is replaced by Larsal's own macOS verifier** in larsal-platform `net` (LD-221 (2)): rustls's ServerCertVerifier over Security.framework, the same OS calls the crate makes on macOS (SecPolicyCreateSSL with the host name, SecTrustCreateWithCertificates, SecTrustEvaluateWithError; revocation as the system does it); signature checks stay rustls's own (verify_tls12_signature, verify_tls13_signature with the provider's algorithms). No crypto is written (rule 3): the OS decides trust. Its MIT copyright line could not be kept out of a copy, and MIT requires it. Tests use a 127.0.0.1 server and a test CA set on the trust object with SecTrustSetAnchorCertificates, never the owner's keychain, never the internet. Two reviews of its unsafe calls (Rust rule 2). (3) **Packages never built on a Mac** (windows-sys, schannel and the rest of the 53 the research marks cut) are vendored as stubs: Cargo.toml and an empty lib.rs, with `.cargo-checksum.json` keeping the package checksum and an empty files list (the practice of cargo-vendor-filterer); a check proves no stub is compiled for aarch64-apple-darwin or x86_64-apple-darwin. (4) **ring's aesni-x86_64.pl is cut** from the crypto copy (LD-221 (3) allows cuts): the crates.io package builds from its pregenerated assembly, never from the .pl generators; an offline x86_64-apple-darwin build proves it. Any such write is a STOP, filed as the next F number. The two other mtime checks (Chrome's plist and its Application Support folder) stay. (3) The 19 Chrome tests may now run on the shared tree. (4) Checker 1 round 3's missing test (a guard hit stops Chrome at once, mutant N2) is added. | Puppeteer and Playwright pass `--use-mock-keychain` on macOS so Chrome never uses the login keychain. securityd logs every commit of a keychain file with the writing process's name and PID (seen in this log). | LD-312 | E7-S1 lead round after Checker 1 round 4; F-211; INT-01.25, INT-01.48, the Limits table, T65, T88; deny.toml | Lead 2026-11-01 | (1) **A size limit in values** (F-211: a 1 MiB flat list made the reader build 16 MiB). Each message may have at most 16,384 commas and opening brackets (`[`, `{`) outside strings, counted together in the same pre-scan as the depth limit, before the message is read; over it, the message is refused with error 12, like one nested too deep (T43 already gives 12 for content that is too complex). Commas alone are not enough: a list of short chains such as `[[[]]]` also costs about 2 bytes per value, and the depth limit allows 32 levels. No request Larsal reads comes near the limit. The check stays in crates/larsal-browser, so larsal-model (E15-S4) is not changed. INT-01.25, the Limits table and new test case T88 say this. (2) T65 runs again for 10 minutes with `-max_len=1048580`, so the fuzzer reaches frames near INT-01.3. The target's box part skips inputs over `MAX_CONTENT + 1`, because its own `seal` of a 1 MiB input would allocate 2 MiB. (3) The Builder also adds Checker 1's probes for M1 to M4 (sibling containers, hello versions that are not whole numbers, no notices for Safari sessions). (4) deny.toml's `crypto_secretbox` entry is deleted too: it was there only for `crypto_box` and is in neither `Cargo.lock`. (5) Then Checker 2 and Verify; no Checker 1 round 5. |

| LD-336 | Three new features: show a password as a QR code, encrypt any file, encrypted folders | Owner 2026-11-02 | The owner asked, word for word: (1) "in every version of the app, include a very obvious quick-access feature for sharing the password as a QR code. (2) "the ability to encrypt a file or an image manually first and place it somewhere else, while still having the same encryption mechanism in the app. [...] I will also be able to encrypt files on demand and send them or save them on my PC, then take them anywhere else and open them with any other app that uses the same encryption. Make this encryption extremely secure and private by studying every possible security method so no one can actually decrypt it." (3) "open a vault in the app that is very similar to Cryptomator [...] encrypts everything very fast, insanely fast, and insanely secure." The lead adds them as E21, E22 and E23 (LD-337). | The owner's product decision. |
| LD-337 | E21, E22 and E23: the lead's technical rulings | Lead 2026-11-02 | (1) **E21, show as QR code.** One action, "Show as QR Code", on every password and every protected field, in every version of Larsal: the Mac app, the `larsal` command line tool, the browser extension and the web vault, and any later version (Windows, Linux, phones) from its first release. It opens a pop-up with a short animation (none when Reduce Motion is on). The code holds the value as plain text; a Wi-Fi entry can also give the standard Wi-Fi join code, so a phone joins the network at once; FEA-04.23's one-time password code stays as it is. Rendering reuses the approved `qrcode` crate (FEA-04.23), so no new crate. The Mac tests read each drawn code back with the system's own QR reader (Core Image), offline. (2) **What a QR code protects, said plainly.** It never goes over a network or through the clipboard, but anyone who sees the screen or has a photo of it can read the password. So the pop-up hides itself after a time and on lock, and Save Image and Share come behind a warning that the image holds the password in readable form. An encrypted share (the code holds a one-time link to Larsal's server, with its key only in the link, never sent to the server) is E21-S6; it waits for hosting (an owner step). (3) **E22, encrypt any file.** The format is age v1 (the public C2SP specification), not a format of Larsal's own, so the files open in any age tool on any system (the owner's "open them with any other app that uses the same encryption"); E22-S1's research checks this pick against the other public formats (OpenPGP RFC 9580 and others) and against every attack it can find, with sources, and the lead rules on its result. The default is a passphrase that Larsal's generator makes and saves as an entry in the vault, so it is never weak and never lost; a public key mode is for sending to other people. Nobody can promise that no one can ever decrypt a file: with Larsal's generated passphrase or a key, no known attack can, and the weak points that are left are a short passphrase a person picks and malware on the computer. The cards say this in the user's words. (4) **E23, encrypted folders.** A folder anywhere (iCloud Drive, Dropbox, a USB stick) holds each file encrypted on its own, with its name encrypted too, so a cloud app uploads only the files that changed. The lead's pick, for E23-S1's research to confirm or overturn with sources: Cryptomator's published vault format, so the same folder also opens in Cryptomator on Windows, Linux and phones; Larsal's code is written from the published specification only, never copied from Cryptomator's code. Speed: AES-256-GCM on the processor's AES instructions, with chunks done in parallel; E23-S1 sets the speed targets from measurements on this Mac. First the files show in Larsal's own window; a Finder location (Apple's File Provider) needs a signed build, so E23-S5 waits for D7 and W-02. No kernel extension. (5) **What the owner is asked first, item by item (rule 7), when each story starts:** the new crypto crates (for E22: `scrypt` and ChaCha20-Poly1305; for E23: AES-SIV and AES key wrap, after AT-J03), the public test vectors, and the age and Cryptomator tools for the cross-checks on demo files. Until a yes, those Checks stay open. (6) **Order.** E21-S1, E22-S1 and E23-S1 are spec work with no code, so they can fill free slots at once. The builds follow the Order lines in plan/epics.md. |
| LD-338 | Passkeys and SSH keys in every version of Larsal | Owner 2026-11-02 | The owner asked, word for word apart from one name: "don't forget to add passkey features, and ssh keys, like [a well-known password manager]. in all types of the app, add it to the plan". The lead adds E24 (LD-339). | The owner's product decision. |
| LD-339 | E24: what the plan already has, and what E24 adds | Lead 2026-11-02 | (1) **Already in the plan:** passkeys kept in the vault and used in the browser through Larsal's extension (E7-S5, INT-04; E7-S7i), passkey sign-in to Larsal's own server (E20-S5), and the SSH agent: keys added to the system agent on unlock and removed on lock, and Larsal's own agent for keys marked "ask first" (E9, Done). (2) **E24 adds, for passkeys:** Larsal as the Mac's passkey provider for Safari and other apps, through Apple's credential provider extension (macOS 14 and later; it needs a signed build, so it waits for D7 and W-02); moving passkeys in and out with the FIDO Alliance's Credential Exchange Format, where macOS offers it; and passkeys listed and managed in the web vault and the `larsal` tool. A private passkey key is never shown or exported in readable form. (3) **E24 adds, for SSH keys:** new SSH keys made in Larsal (Ed25519 by default); Larsal's own agent for every key, with an approval for each new program that asks (Touch ID once E6-S3 has a signed build); signing git commits with an SSH key through a small `larsal` signing program in OpenSSH's signature format, so `git verify-commit` and `ssh-keygen -Y verify` accept it; the public key filled into a site's "add SSH key" form by the extension; and the public parts shown and copied in the web vault and the `larsal` tool. (4) **Every version.** As LD-337 (1): the Mac app, the `larsal` tool, the extension and the web vault each get the parts they can run, and any later version (Windows, Linux, phones) gets them from its first release. (5) **Safety rules stay.** No agent or test reads ~/.ssh or talks to the owner's ssh-agent: tests use demo keys, throwaway HOME folders and throwaway agents (kickoff rules). Importing a key reads only a file the user picks. E24-S1's research checks every pick against public sources (W3C WebAuthn Level 3, the FIDO Credential Exchange specifications, Apple's AuthenticationServices documentation, OpenSSH's agent and signature protocols, git's documentation) and the lead rules on its result. |
| LD-340 | Zero-knowledge is Larsal's core promise | Owner 2026-11-02, lead | (1) The owner said: "Don't forget the core thing in our app is zero-knowledge encryption not end-to-end encryption." (2) **What it means for Larsal.** Zero-knowledge: no server of ours, and no one who runs or breaks into one, holds anything that can open a vault, a file or a key, or test a guess at a password. End-to-end encryption only protects data on its way between two ends; zero-knowledge also covers what the server stores, the sign-in, recovery and key changes, against a server that is hostile (the threat model of the ETH Zurich study in spec/research/sync-server.md). (3) **The plan already holds it** (plan/architecture.md, "What the server sees"): no password reaches the server in any form, not even a hash or a verifier (SYN-04); the account blob needs a 256-bit secret only the user's devices hold (SYN-06.4); the server never links the vault reader (SYN-02.9, the "server blind" line); keys are signed and pinned so the server cannot swap them; there is no reset that lets us open a vault (the Recovery Kit is the user's). (4) **Every feature follows it, new ones included:** E21-S6's link keeps its key in the part of the link the server never gets; E22 and E23 work on the Mac, and a cloud sees only encrypted files; E24's passkeys and SSH private keys never leave the vault in readable form; the web vault's served-code limit stays named (WEB-02). A feature that would break the rule is not built. (5) **Words.** Larsal's own text calls this zero-knowledge, in plain words: "We can't see your passwords, files or keys. Only you hold them." "End-to-end" is never the main claim. (6) **Proof.** E13-S8 proves it with tests: the full server store, its logs and every request, read in full, must not hold any demo secret and must not let anyone check a password guess. |

A cleanup warning is necessary failure reporting, never a cancellation PASS. | Original acceptance is unchanged and remains unproved in S3/S4; candidate custody/cleanup and later I/O/crash behavior need implementation evidence. |

| LD-369 | E22-S1 R4; reviewed specification only | Lead closure record 2026-11-05; reviewed specification only | New-name-only output, explicit chosen destination, no replace, no auto-open, generated credential saved durably before encryption; manual password/recipient-only encryption on explicit choice. | Existing files and originals survive. Generated credential may remain if encryption later fails. |
| LD-370 | E22-S1 R5; reviewed specification only | Lead closure record 2026-11-05; reviewed specification only | S3 vectors enumerate all published tests: profile-supported expectations, policy-limit/unsupported expectations recorded separately; no unsupported refusal counts as interoperability PASS. | Original “every vector passes” remains OPEN pending formal mapping/adoption; future runtime proof not an S1 measurement gate. |
| LD-371 | E22-S1 R6; reviewed specification only | Lead closure record 2026-11-05; reviewed specification only | E22 creates a portable local file and contains no network, upload or Larsal-server storage path. LD-340's no-password-verifier obligation applies to every Larsal service and remains unchanged; the owner-chosen USB, cloud and email destinations expressly authorized by E22 do not create a Larsal service. Anyone obtaining a portable passphrase-encrypted file can test guesses offline, so a weak chosen phrase remains unsafe. No third-party-storage no-guess promise is made. Any later Larsal service that stores or transports these files requires a reviewed design satisfying LD-340 before that feature is built. | Local portable output is already authorized; no new service route or waiver of any Larsal-service promise is proposed. |

## E23-S1 adopted specification rulings

R1–7 are recorded exactly after seven second-Spec corrections and fresh independent Verify APPROVE for specification/static proposals only. LD-372–378 record R1–7 exactly; AT-FOLD-01–16 and FOLD-P1–5 are final catalog identifiers with all runtime proof OPEN/todo. LD-364/365 remain reserved. No original acceptance/Order or other story status changes. Original dependencies, technical/sensitive reviews and E23-S2 itemized AT-J03/package/tool/corpus download gates govern implementation. Exact dependency/license/advisory/checksum closure remains unresolved where uncomputed; no API/download grant or runtime PASS. Owner frame review and W-18 wording remain nonblocking under LD-220/268/363; no new permission gate or owner frame approval. Conditional adoption/closure proof: the encrypted-folder research Review and proof footer.

| ID | Exact adopted specification ruling | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| LD-372 (R1) | Adopt classic8-gcm-local-1 as the initial researched profile. All original create/unlock/list/read/write/rename/delete, parallel-GCM and successful foreign-app interoperability capabilities remain required and OPEN. Profile refusals never count as those successes. UVF spec1 at F09 is studied, unimplemented and not silently substituted. | F01–03, F09–17 |
| LD-373 (R2) | Retain original E23-S3 “one changed byte gives the generic error and no plaintext” unchanged and OPEN. Complete stable-input validation precedes any plaintext for malformed structure or failed authentication; require valid controls and individual changed header/nonce/payload/tag bytes with zero early plaintext. Authentic whole-object substitution, replay and whole trailing-chunk removal are separate S6 handling/disclosure limits, not evidence of a contradiction in that narrower S3 check. Known-history mismatch stops automatic use; no universal freshness/name-content binding. Enumerate unauthenticated representations separately; an exact one-byte counterexample needs its object/mutation and a narrow Lead ruling, never a blanket waiver or acceptance replacement. | F01–03/F05, E22 custody |
| LD-374 (R3) | Retain original S4 functional Open, closed-file/no-leftover and lock-closes-files checks unchanged and OPEN. Lead selects first-party Larsal file/document views and handles, with no readable disk copy for Open. Rust retains bounded owned content and exact byte access for arbitrary supported regular files; text, image, PDF and binary cases require meaningful presentation, with byte view supplementary to required semantic previews. Close and every enabled lifecycle trigger revoke all live views/handles and wipe owned buffers. Each named rendering/data route needs the API/secret review inventory in FIL-05 and S4 runtime proof, including cache/index/disk absence and transient UI lifetimes; no passwords/keys enter Swift. Withdraw external Open Copy/Finish and the external-custody exception. Save Out is persistent readable export; arbitrary-app Finder remains separate S5 with signing. Refusal, warning or hex-only substitution never passes a required success. | F43–49, APP-10, LD-220 |
| LD-375 (R4) | Adopt FIL-06's bounded resource, durable local ciphertext recovery and deterministic keep-both policy. Two Macs with observable competing versions preserve both without clock-based overwrite; a provider that destroys/withholds the only other version cannot be made lossless by this format. Proposed exact concurrency qualification: “No silent loss of any version observed or durably created by Larsal; unseen provider deletion and forks are disclosed availability limits.” Recovery retains original encrypted names plus parent IDs, no clear names/content in raw objects/journal bodies/filenames/logs; test marker absence after lock/restart and name/version recovery after unlock. No cloud lock file is a distributed lock. | APP-05, F13/F37/F38 |
| LD-376 (R5) | Route every new crypto primitive through larsal-crypto, retain existing RNG, secret types, allocator and page-lock boundary. Candidate packages/configurations below need original E23-S2 AT-J03, itemized owner download approval and full closure/license/advisory review. Enforce RFC7914 N<2^(16*r) before allocation/KDF and both content/shared-header GCM lifetime budgets (FIL-06.16), including unknown-peer-history limits. No package, tool, checksum, corpus or extra API crossing is approved by this draft. | F18–29, architecture |
| LD-377 (R6) | E23 operates locally on an owner-selected portable storage folder. LD-371's offline-guess disclosure applies by the same portable-storage reasoning; LD-340's every-Larsal-service no-verifier/server-blind promise is unchanged. No E23 server path exists. Own-window work follows original dependencies; Finder remains S5 after S4, D7 and W-02, with File Provider cache/custody proof; FSKit is a later macOS15.4+ alternative, no kernel extension. | LD-340/371, F20/F30–35 |
| LD-378 (R7) | Adopt the measured-filesystem-derived target methodology below for future E23-S3 release measurements. S1's cache-influenced raw filesystem results are not encryption, interop, memory or large-file proof. Preserve all original S3 speed checks and the original story Order/statuses. | Owned baseline; F21/F27 |
